# Editorial Audit — *Revenge of the Iron-Blooded Sword Hound*

**Source reviewed:** `Revenge of the Iron-Blooded Sword Hound (Info Windows and Grammar Corrected).epub`  
**Scope:** 500 chapters, 65,912 body paragraphs, approximately 844,000 English words  
**Deliverable:** Editorial adjustment list only; the EPUB itself has not been altered by this audit.

> **Important limitation:** No Korean raw text was supplied. Items labeled **Potential mistranslation / Korean-source verification** are evidence-based suspicions raised by broken semantics, literal Korean-style syntax, inconsistent referents, or implausible word choice. They are not confirmed translations. A definitive retranslation requires the corresponding Korean passages.

## Editorial assessment

The manuscript is readable at the plot level, but it is not yet publication-ready English. It requires a full line edit rather than another isolated search-and-replace pass. The most important remaining problems are:

1. **Broken or incomplete sentences:** missing predicates, tense changes, sentence fragments, and absent terminal punctuation.
2. **Machine-translated syntax:** literal clause order, abstract filler nouns such as “figure,” “appearance,” “state,” and “being,” and Korean-style transitions that sound unnatural in English.
3. **Semantic instability:** occasional implausible nouns, incorrect referents, gender shifts, and phrases whose intended meaning cannot be recovered safely without the Korean source.
4. **Mechanical inconsistency:** punctuation spacing, fused words, duplicated words, quotation-mark imbalance, and inconsistent treatment of system/information text.
5. **Terminology drift:** overlapping labels for houses/clans, monster ratings, sword ranks, places, and capitalized fantasy terms.
6. **Register drift:** narration moves between formal epic prose, casual modern speech, profanity, game terminology, and literal web-translation phrasing without a stable editorial standard.

## Recommended house style before line editing

- **Narrative tense:** Use past tense consistently, except for universal truths, direct thoughts deliberately written in present tense, and system text.
- **Point of view:** Keep third-person references stable. Remove accidental first-person intrusions unless they belong to direct thought or dialogue.
- **Dialogue:** Retain curly quotation marks. Put punctuation inside the closing quotation mark. Keep dialogue paragraphs indented under the existing EPUB design.
- **Thoughts:** Decide whether single quotation marks indicate direct thought everywhere. Apply the same treatment consistently.
- **Ellipses:** Use the single character `…`. Do not mix it with `...`, spaced dots, or repeated ellipses. Use spacing according to function: no internal spaces; one following space when a new sentence continues.
- **Sound effects and vocal elongation:** Decide whether Korean-style romanized SFX and extended spellings such as `Kuaaah` remain. If retained, standardize their punctuation and capitalization; otherwise localize them into natural English sound effects.
- **Scene breaks:** Use `◆◆◆` exclusively and keep the ornament outside information windows and dialogue classes.
- **Numbers:** Spell out small narrative numbers where appropriate; retain numerals for levels, ranks, measurements, dates, scores, and system windows. Standardize unit spacing (`7 m`, `20 kg`) unless the chosen style guide specifies closed units.
- **Capitalization:** Capitalize official institutions, named wars, factions, ranks used as titles before names, and unique system terms only when they are genuinely proper nouns. Avoid random capitalization of common monster classes and generic roles.
- **Romanization:** Maintain one canonical spelling for every person, clan, location, demon, weapon, and technique. Keep a locked terminology sheet beside the manuscript during editing.
- **Profanity/register:** Decide whether the target edition is uncensored. If profanity remains, keep each character’s register coherent rather than alternating between formal and crude phrasing unpredictably.

### Terms that should be checked against the Korean source

- **Sword Graduator:** This is not standard English fantasy terminology. Confirm the Korean rank and decide whether to retain it as a coined title or localize it as *Sword Graduate*, *Sword Adept*, or another established tier.
- **Supreme realm / master level:** Establish whether these are formal ranks. If formal, capitalize and hyphenate consistently; if descriptive, keep them lowercase.
- **Danger Rating / Risk Level / Threat Level:** Select one label for monster-profile windows unless the Korean text distinguishes different systems.
- **House / Clan / family:** Define whether each Korean term maps to a different political or genealogical concept. Do not flatten them without checking the source.
- **Le Rouge Et Le Noir Mountain / Red and Black Mountains:** Determine whether one is an official translated name and the other an informal description, or whether they are accidental duplicates.
- **Demon / demonic / Demonic:** Capitalize only when part of an official species, faction, title, or named class.

## Recommended production sequence

1. Obtain or align the Korean raws chapter by chapter, especially for every **Critical** and **Potential mistranslation** item.
2. Freeze the glossary for names, houses, ranks, locations, demons, skills, weapons, and system-window labels.
3. Perform a human retranslation pass on semantically unstable passages before copyediting their English grammar.
4. Complete a chapter-by-chapter line edit for grammar, syntax, register, paragraph flow, and dialogue attribution.
5. Run a mechanical proof after the prose edit: quotes, ellipses, dashes, capitalization, spacing, numbers, and units.
6. Rebuild the EPUB and perform device-level visual QA on information windows, scene breaks, dialogue clusters, volume pages, navigation, and side stories.

---

*This audit intentionally distinguishes definite English corrections from uncertain translation questions. Any item marked low or medium confidence should be reviewed in narrative context rather than applied blindly.*
